# U 開頭詞條

<a id="entry-u-000001"></a>
## U

**發音：** /juː/
*prop. n.* 1. **濮滿 /濮滿** — An Austroasiatic language spoken in China.

<a id="entry-u-000002"></a>
## u

*n.* 1. **鈾** — a heavy toxic silvery-white radioactive metallic element; occurs in many isotopes; used for nuclear fuels and nuclear weapons
2. a base containing nitrogen that is found in RNA (but not in DNA) and derived from pyrimidine; pairs with adenine
3. the 21st letter of the Roman alphabet
*adj.* 1. (chiefly British) of or appropriate to the upper classes especially in language use

<a id="entry-u-000003"></a>
## U-boat

*n.* 1. *(historical)* **U艇** — Any German submarine of the First or Second World War, or any Austro-Hungarian submarine of the First World War.

<a id="entry-u-000004"></a>

*n.* 1. **租用汽車** — a rented car
*“she picked up a hire car at the airport and drove to her hotel”*

## u-shaped

*adj.* 1. **U形狀+的** — shaped in the form of the letter U

<a id="entry-u-000006"></a>
## U-turn

*n.* 1. **轉頭 /轉頭, 調頭 /調頭, 掉頭 /掉頭, U轉 /U轉** — A turn in a vehicle carried out by driving in a semicircle in order to travel in the opposite direction.
2. *(figuratively)* **髮夾彎 /髮夾彎, 大轉彎 /大轉彎** — A reversal of policy or opinion.

<a id="entry-u-000007"></a>

<a id="entry-u-000042"></a>
## ugly

**發音：** /ˈʌɡli/
*adj.* 1. **醜+的, 醜陋+的, 令人厭惡+的, 媸, 難看+的** — displeasing to the senses
*“an ugly face”*
*“ugly furniture”*
2. **難看 /難看, 醜 /醜, 媸, 醜陋 /醜陋** — Displeasing to the eye; aesthetically unpleasing.
3. **難聽 /難聽** — Displeasing to the ear or some other sense.
4. inclined to anger or bad feelings with overtones of menace
*“a surly waiter”*
*“an ugly frame of mind”*
5. morally reprehensible
*“would do something as despicable as murder”*
*“ugly crimes”*
6. provoking horror
*“an atrocious automobile accident”*
*“a frightful crime of decapitation”*

*“the ultimate achievement”*
*“the ultimate question”*

*“the ultimate sonata of that opus”*
*“a distinction between the verb and noun senses of `conflict' is that in the verb the stress is on the ultimate (or last) syllable”*

## unique

**發音：** /juːˈniːk/
*adj.* 1. **無與倫比+的, 無比+的, 無敵+的, 獨一無二+的, 獨特+的** — radically distinctive and without equal
*“he is alone in the field of microbiology”*
*“this theory is altogether alone in its penetration of the problem”*
2. *(not-comparable)* **獨一無二 /獨一無二, 唯一, 特異 /特異** — Being the only one of its kind; unequaled, unparalleled or unmatched.
3. **唯一+的, 獨一無二+的** — the single one of its kind
*“a singular example”*
*“the unique existing example of Donne's handwriting”*
4. (followed by `to') applying exclusively to a given category or condition or locality
*“a species unique to Australia”*
5. highly unusual or rare but not the single instance
*“spoke with a unique accent”*
*“had unique ability in raising funds”*

3. *(uncountable, usually)* **同音, 齊唱 /齊唱, 齊奏 /齊奏** — Identical pitch between two notes or sounds; the simultaneous playing of notes of identical pitch (or separated by one or more octaves).
4. occurring together or simultaneously

## unit

**發音：** /ˈjuː.nɪt/
*n.* 1. **單位** — any division of quantity accepted as a standard of measurement or exchange
*“the dollar is the United States unit of currency”*
*“a unit of wheat is a bushel”*
2. **單元, 單位** — an individual or group or structure or other entity regarded as a structural or functional constituent of a whole
*“the reduced the number of units and installations”*
*“the word is a basic linguistic unit”*
3. **單元, 單位** — a single undivided whole
*“an idea is not a unit that can be moved from one brain to another”*
4. **部件** — An organized group comprising people or equipment.
5. **部件, 單位** — an assemblage of parts that is regarded as a single entity
*“how big is that part compared to the whole?”*
*“the team is a unit”*
6. **單位 /單位** — A standard measure of a quantity or magnitude.

3. a behavioral convention or pattern characteristic of all members of a particular culture or of all human beings

*“in motor vehicles a universal joint allows the driveshaft to move up and down as the vehicle passes over bumps”*

*v.* 1. **使不適, 使心煩, 擾亂, 打擾** — move deeply
*“This book upset me”*
*“A troubling thought”*
2. *(transitive)* **刺激** — To make (a person) angry, distressed, or unhappy.
3. **打翻, 推翻, 顛覆, 翻倒, 翻轉** — cause to overturn from an upright or normal position
*“The cat knocked over the flower vase”*
*“the clumsy customer turned over the vase”*
4. disturb the balance or stability of
*“The hostile talks upset the peaceful relations between the two countries”*
5. cause to lose one's composure
6. form metals with a swage
*n.* 1. **不高興, 心煩, 擾動, 擾亂** — an unhappy and worried mental state
*“there was too much anger and disturbance”*
*“she didn't realize the upset she caused me”*
2. **病理狀態, 不適, 失調** — a physical condition in which there is a disturbance of normal functioning
*“the doctor prescribed some medicine for the disorder”*
*“everyone gets stomach upsets from time to time”*
3. **傾覆, 弄翻, 推翻, 翻倒, 翻覆** — the act of upsetting something
*“he was badly bruised by the upset of his sled at a high speed”*
4. the act of disturbing the mind or body
*“his carelessness could have caused an ecological upset”*
*“she was unprepared for this sudden overthrow of their normal way of living”*
5. a tool used to thicken or spread metal (the end of a bar or a rivet etc.) by forging or hammering or swaging
6. an improbable and unexpected victory
*“the biggest upset since David beat Goliath”*
*adj.* 1. **使不安+的, 焦急+的, 著急+的, 悶悶不樂+的, 憂慮+的** — afflicted with or marked by anxious uneasiness or trouble or grief
*“too upset to say anything”*
*“spent many disquieted moments”*
2. *(predicative)* **懊惱 /懊惱** — Angry, distressed, or unhappy
3. **不適** — mildly physically distressed
*“an upset stomach”*
4. **翻過來+的** — having been turned so that the bottom is no longer the bottom
*“an overturned car”*
*“the upset pitcher of milk”*
5. thrown into a state of disarray or confusion
*“troops fleeing in broken ranks”*
*“a confused mass of papers on the desk”*
6. used of an unexpected defeat of a team favored to win
*“the Bills' upset victory over the Houston Oilers”*

## usable

*adj.* 1. **可用+的, 有用+的** — capable of being put to use

3. **可使用的, 可操作的, 合用+的, 能用+的, 起作用的** — fit or ready for use or service
*“the toaster was still functional even after being dropped”*

*“the house is available after July 1”*

<a id="entry-u-001466"></a>

<a id="entry-u-001470"></a>
## use

**發音：** /juːs/
*v.* 1. *(transitive)* **用, 使用, 應用 /應用** — To employ; to apply; to utilize.
2. **使用, 利用, 運用** — seek or achieve an end by using to one's advantage
*“She uses her influential friends to get jobs”*
*“The president's wife used her good connections”*
3. **應用, 用, 運用, 適用, 使用** — avail oneself to
*“apply a principle”*
*“practice a religion”*
4. **慣常, 曾經習慣** — habitually do something (use only in the past tense)
*“She used to call her mother every week but now she calls only occasionally”*
*“I used to get sick when I ate in that dining hall”*
5. put into service; make work or employ for a particular purpose or for its inherent or natural purpose
*“use your head!”*
*“we only use Spanish at home”*
6. take or consume (regularly or habitually)
*“She uses drugs rarely”*
*n.* 1. **作用, 機能, 功能** — what something is used for
*“the function of an auger is to bore holes”*
*“ballet is beautiful but what use is it?”*
2. **消費** — (economics) the utilization of economic goods to satisfy needs or in manufacturing
*“the consumption of energy has increased steadily”*
3. **習慣** — (psychology) an automatic pattern of behavior in reaction to a specific situation; may be inherited or acquired through frequent repetition
*“owls have nocturnal habits”*
*“she had a habit twirling the ends of her hair”*
4. *(countable, uncountable)* **用, 使用, 利用** — The act of using.
5. the act of using
*“he warned against the use of narcotic drugs”*
*“skilled in the utilization of computers”*
6. a particular service
*“he put his knowledge to good use”*
*“patrons have their uses”*

<a id="entry-u-001471"></a>

<a id="entry-u-001514"></a>
## usual

**發音：** /ˈjuː.ʒʊəl/
*adj.* 1. **常見+的, 通常+的, 普通+的** — occurring or encountered or experienced or observed frequently or in accordance with regular practice or procedure
*“grew the usual vegetables”*
*“the usual summer heat”*
2. **通常** — Most commonly occurring; typical.
3. commonly encountered
*“a common (or familiar) complaint”*
*“the usual greeting”*

<a id="entry-u-001515"></a>
## usually

**發音：** /ˈjuːʒʊəli/
*adv.* 1. **通常** — Most of the time; less than always, but more than occasionally.
2. under normal conditions
*“usually she was late”*

<a id="entry-u-001516"></a>
## usufruct

**發音：** /ˈjuːz(j)ʊfɹʌkt/
*n.* 1. **用益物權 /用益物權** — The legal right to use and derive profit or benefit from property that belongs to another person, as long as the property is not damaged.
2. a legal right to use and derive profit from property belonging to someone else provided that the property itself is not injured in any way

<a id="entry-u-001517"></a>
## usufructuary

*n.* 1. someone who holds property by usufruct
*adj.* 1. **使用收益權+的, 有用益權+的** — of or relating to the nature of a usufruct

<a id="entry-u-001518"></a>
## usurer

**發音：** /ˈjuːʒəɹə/
*n.* 1. **高利貸 /高利貸** — A person who loans money to others and charges interest, particularly at an exorbitant, exploitative, or illegal rate.
2. someone who lends money at excessive rates of interest

<a id="entry-u-001519"></a>
## usurp

**發音：** /juˈsɝp/
*v.* 1. **接手, 篡奪, 霸佔, 佔領, 奪取** — seize and take control without authority and possibly with force; take as one's right or possession
*“He assumed to himself the right to fill all positions in the town”*
*“he usurped my rights”*
2. **篡奪 /篡奪** — To seize and hold or use (powers, an office, a coat of arms, a right or copyright, etc) from another, without right (usually by illegitimate means).
3. **奪取, 篡奪** — take the place of
*“gloom had usurped mirth at the party after the news of the terrorist act broke”*

<a id="entry-u-001520"></a>
## usurpation

**發音：** /ˌjuː.sə(ɹ)ˈpeɪ.ʃən/
*n.* 1. *(countable, uncountable)* **篡位** — The wrongful seizure of something by force, especially of sovereignty or other authority.
2. entry to another's property without right or permission
3. wrongfully seizing and holding (an office or powers) by force (especially the seizure of a throne or supreme authority)
*“a succession of generals who ruled by usurpation”*

<a id="entry-u-001521"></a>
## usurper

**發音：** /juˈsɜɹ.pəɹ/
*n.* 1. **僭主, 篡位者, 篡奪者 /篡奪者, 篡權者 /篡權者** — One who usurps.
2. one who wrongfully or illegally seizes and holds the place of another

<a id="entry-u-001522"></a>
## usury

**發音：** /ˈjuːʒəɹi/
*n.* 1. *(countable)* **高利** — An exorbitant rate of interest, in excess of any legal rates or at least immorally.
2. **重利, 高利, 高利貸** — an exorbitant or unlawful rate of interest
3. the act of lending money at an exorbitant rate of interest

## utilitarian

**發音：** /juːˌtɪlɪˈtɛəɹi.ən/
*adj.* 1. **實用+的, 有用+的** — having utility often to the exclusion of values
*“plain utilitarian kitchenware”*
2. **功利主義 /功利主義** — Practical and functional, present for use, not just for show.
3. having a useful function
*“utilitarian steel tables”*
*n.* 1. someone who believes that the value of a thing depends on its utility

<a id="entry-u-001529"></a>
## utilitarianism

*n.* 1. *(countable, uncountable)* **效益主義 /效益主義, 功利主義 /功利主義** — The theory that action should be directed toward achieving the "greatest happiness for the greatest number of people" (hedonistic universalism), or one of various related theories.
2. doctrine that the useful is the good; especially as elaborated by Jeremy Bentham and James Mill; the aim was said to be the greatest happiness for the greatest number

<a id="entry-u-001530"></a>
## utility

**發音：** /juːˈtɪlɪti/
*n.* 1. *(countable, uncountable)* **效用, 用處 /用處** — The state or condition of being useful; usefulness.
2. **效用** — (economics) a measure that is to be maximized in any situation involving choice
3. a company that performs a public service; subject to government regulation
4. the quality of being of practical use
5. the service (electric power or water or transportation) provided by a public utility
*“the cost of utilities never decreases”*
*“all the utilities were lost after the hurricane”*
6. (computer science) a program designed for general support of the processes of a computer
*“a computer system provides utility programs to perform the tasks needed by most users”*
*adj.* 1. used of beef; usable but inferior
2. capable of substituting in any of several positions on a team
*“a utility infielder”*

## utmost

**發音：** /ˈʌtməʊ̯st/
*adj.* 1. *(not-comparable)* **極度 /極度, 極 /極, 最大** — The most extreme; greatest, ultimate.
2. of the greatest possible degree or extent or intensity
*“extreme cold”*
*“extreme caution”*
3. highest in extent or degree
*“to the last measure of human endurance”*
*“whether they were accomplices in the last degree or a lesser one was...to be determined individually”*
4. (comparatives of `far') most remote in space or time or order
*“had traveled to the farthest frontier”*
*“don't go beyond the farthermost (or furthermost) tree”*
*n.* 1. the greatest possible degree
*“he tried his utmost”*

## utopia

**發音：** /juːˈtəʊ.pi.ə/
*n.* 1. *(countable, uncountable)* **烏託邦 /烏托邦, 桃花源, 烏有鄉 /烏有鄉** — A world in which everything and everyone works in perfect harmony.
2. **烏托邦, 理想國, 桃花源** — ideally perfect state; especially in its social and political and moral aspects
3. a book written by Sir Thomas More (1516) describing the perfect society on an imaginary island
4. a work of fiction describing a utopia
5. an imaginary place considered to be perfect or ideal

## utter

**發音：** /ˈʌtə/
*v.* 1. **做聲, 發出** — express audibly; utter sounds (not necessarily words)
*“She let out a big heavy sigh”*
*“He uttered strange sounds that nobody could understand”*
2. *(transitive)* **發出 /發出, 說出 /說出** — Sometimes preceded by forth, out, etc.: to produce (a cry, speech, or other sounds) with the voice.
3. **流通** — put into circulation
*“utter counterfeit currency”*
4. articulate; either verbally or with a cry, shout, or noise
*“She expressed her anger”*
*“He uttered a curse”*
5. express in speech
*“She talks a lot of nonsense”*
*“This depressed patient does not verbalize”*
*adj.* 1. without qualification; used informally as (often pejorative) intensifiers
*“an arrant fool”*
*“a complete coward”*
2. complete
*“came to a dead stop”*
*“utter seriousness”*

<a id="entry-u-001547"></a>
## uttered

*adj.* 1. **以言語表現+的** — communicated in words
*“frequently uttered sentiments”*

<a id="entry-u-001548"></a>
## utterer

*n.* 1. **講演者, 說話的人** — someone who expresses in language; someone who talks (especially someone who delivers a public speech or someone especially garrulous)
*“the speaker at commencement”*
*“an utterer of useful maxims”*
2. an organism that can utter vocal sounds
*“an utterer of foul oaths”*
*“is the giraffe a vocalizer?”*
3. someone who circulates forged banknotes or counterfeit coins
